### India Targets Journalists in Crackdown on Growing Protest Movement
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-19 21:20 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/gen-z-india-protests-journalists-press-freedom-new-delhi-cockroach-janata-party-demonstrations-youth-journalists-detained-jailed/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

As protests continue across India over allegations of government corruption, dozens of members of the media have been subjected to arrest, detainment and assault, according to witnesses, press rights organizations, and the journalists themselves. Thousands of protesters have taken to the streets of New Delhi in recent weeks, demanding the resignation of election officials allegedly tied to the deletion of nearly 130 million names from voter registration rolls. The protests — which have largely been organized by the Cockroach Janata Party, so named after India's chief justice called the nation’s unemployed youth and activists "cockroaches" — are part of a wider wave of youth discontent aimed at Prime Minister Narendra Modi and his administration. Organizers have mobilized young people via social media, tapping into frustrations with government corruption, eroding trust in democratic institutions, and a lack of access to jobs and educational opportunities.

### Testing the Legal Limits of Press Access
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-15 14:51 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/press-access-trump-white-house-press-pool-political-cnn-reporting-president-justice-department-judge/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

The Trump administration’s latest attacks on the news media have followed a familiar pattern: President Donald Trump lashes out at news outlets for perceived bias and follows up with restrictive actions that appear blatantly unconstitutional; a judge’s ruling follows, temporarily halting the actions; and the administration pivots and pushes ahead with other restrictions. This time it was the removal of three outlets — CNN, MS NOW, and Politico — from White House press access in September. After the groups sued, and U.S. District Judge Timothy Kelly issued a temporary restraining order restoring their access on Sept. 24, the administration barred CNN and Politico from providing pool coverage on trips outside Washington. Kelly issued a longer, preliminary injunction on Oct. 14, halting the administration from blocking the news organizations’ access to the White House while the case proceeds. Meanwhile, in another escalation involving TV pool coverage of the White House, the Justice Department announced Oct.

### This Newsroom Wants You to Be Part of the Journey Before It Even Starts
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-06 10:00 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/independent-journalism-new-newsroom-launch-otter-news-daniel-drepper-business-plan-climate-crisis-environmental-journalism/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

My happiest times in journalism have been when I have built something new. For my latest venture, I want to help bring investigative journalism on climate and environmental issues to a much larger audience than it has reached in the past. To do so, I’m starting a brand-new newsroom focused on the environmental and climate crisis, and I’m taking my audience along for the ride. I’ll be documenting every facet of the process with an “open kitchen” approach of radical transparency, starting with a 100-day video countdown chronicling all the steps involved with starting a news organization from the ground up. This effort combines two of the things I’m most passionate about: I love digging up stories that other people don’t want to see published, and I love creating opportunities for other journalists. I’ve been a journalist for most of my life. I started working at age 14 for the local paper in my rural German town, turned to doing investigative stories in my early 20s, and later studied investigative journalism at the Columbia University Graduate School of Journalism in New York.

### America’s Shrinking Local Newsrooms, Through a Photographer’s Lens
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-02 12:30 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/americas-shrinking-local-newsrooms-photographer-ann-hermes-local-news-news-deserts-rural-america/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For many journalists, the sight of empty desks is a sadly familiar one. My first glimpse of a gutted newsroom facing further cutbacks sparked a documentary photography project that has so far taken me to more than 50 newsrooms across the United States over the past eight years. It began when my mother, a loyal subscriber to our local metro in southern Illinois, told me a decade ago how disappointed she was in the newspaper. She complained that the local coverage was lacking, and the paper seemed to be thinner. Already several years into my photojournalism career, I was unsurprised by her remarks — I had been witnessing the decline of print media since graduation from journalism school. Still, my curiosity was piqued, and I arranged a visit to the paper to see for myself. Cue the scene of a barren newsroom. Not only were there empty desks, but also an expanse of exposed carpet with indentations showing where desks had once long stood. While there has been a great deal of reporting on the issues plaguing local journalism, I hadn’t seen the story told visually.

### Seven Takeaways on Convenience and News
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-25 14:53 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/gabe-bullard-against-convenience-book-technology-friction-news-online-social-media/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

"Against Convenience: Embracing Friction in an Age of Endless Ease" was released on Sept.15, 2026, by Hanover Square Press, an imprint of HarperCollins Publishers. Courtesy of Hanover Square Press From the earliest pitch, I knew that I wanted to cover the news industry in my book, “Against Convenience.” The book looks at the technology around us and asks whether it’s really improving our lives. Is delivery food worth the higher price? Is streaming music better than collecting CDs or MP3s? News embodies the paradox of modern convenience: We have access to more information than ever before, but misinformation and partisanship are rampant and the purveyors of reliable information are struggling to stay solvent. Plus, I figured if I was going to criticize other fields — tech, entertainment, appliance manufacturing — I couldn’t avoid my own. I don’t know how interested the average nonjournalist is in the day-to-day operations of the news business, so I tried to look in the mirror more than gaze at my navel.

### The YouTuber Shaking Up Brazil’s Powerful Sports Media
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-21 17:04 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/caze-tv-casimiro-miguel-fifa-world-cup-twitch-youtube-streamer-sports-journalism-tv-globo-brazil/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For more than half a century, Brazil’s TV Globo was the undisputed home of soccer in a nation obsessed with it. That dominance is now being tested by Casimiro Miguel, known as “Cazé” — a 32-year-old journalism school dropout who has built a massive online following and shaken up the Brazilian sports broadcasting landscape. Powered by features like live chat and extended pre- and post-game analysis, CazéTV has accomplished what traditional television networks dream of: pulling in new viewers and keeping them engaged for hours. FIFA, soccer’s global governing body, describes CazéTV “the top digital sports platform in Brazil” and calls it “central to its content strategy.” That growth has come with controversy and accusations of misogyny and a lack of diversity. Brazilian regulators are investigating suspected predatory advertising practices by CazéTV. Still, Nicolas Duprat, a Brazilian sports media researcher who has studied CazéTV for years, credits the channel with helping democratize access for fans who can’t afford stadium tickets or a TV Globo subscription.

Critics this morning are lambasting the “repressive” measures. What protests are planned? Both student groups and trade unions have planned rallies across the country. France’s ⁠main student union, UNEF, has called for demonstrations on Saturday and the Yellow Vest movement also filed plans to march through central Paris. There are already overnight curfews in place in Paris and many other cities. In Marseille and Lyon, the second and third-largest cities, all demonstrations have been banned for the weekend. The Marseille city government also initially said it would stop public transport running on Saturday; they’ve hastily reversed that severely unpopular decision this morning. In Paris, protesters have been told they can only march a designated route between the Bastille and Nation squares.

### Severe flooding in eastern Spain, while UK hit by first named storm of winter
Source: The Guardian
Published: 2026-10-23 08:16 UTC
URL: https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/oct/23/spain-floods-storm-adam-uk-storm-austen
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Storm Adam brings heavy rain to Valencia, Alicante and Balearic Islands, as Storm Austen gusts strike England and Wales Storm Adam brought torrential rain to eastern Spain earlier this week. In Pego, Alicante, 632mm fell in 36 hours with 430mm of this falling in just six hours, while the Valencian town of Olivia received 600mm in 24 hours. As a result, 145 municipalities, approximately 850,000 people, across Valencia and Alicante were placed in a temporary lockdown on Monday due to the severe flooding. At least five people have died and 500 have been rescued and 2,100 evacuated. The storm also affected the Balearic Islands, with more than 130 incidents recorded in Ibiza including standing water at the airport, which caused some flights to be diverted or suspended. Although the worst of the storm has passed and conditions improving across Valencia and Alicante, local disruption is expected to continue. Continue reading...

URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178049
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

据新华社12月15日援引澳大利亚广播公司报道，澳大利亚联合反恐小组调查人员表示，悉尼邦迪滩枪击事件两名嫌疑人曾宣誓效忠“伊斯兰国”恐怖组织。 报道援引联合反恐小组一名不愿透露姓名的高级官员的话说，警方在邦迪滩两名枪手车内发现一面“伊斯兰国”旗帜。澳大利亚安全情报组织6年前就已在调查邦迪滩枪击案两名枪手之一的纳维德·阿克拉姆，他与“伊斯兰国”在悉尼的恐怖分子有密切联系。 澳大利亚警方表示，枪击事件的两名嫌疑人是父子关系，使用合法获得的枪支发动了此次袭击事件。 澳大利亚总理阿尔巴尼斯15日表示：“今天下午4点， 我将把更严格的枪支法律列入国家内阁的议程，包括限制个人可持有或许可的枪支数量 。此外，还需对许可证进行定期审查。人们的情况会发生变化，人们的思想也会随着时间的推移而变得激进。许可证不应永久有效。” 此次枪击事件已造成包括1名作案嫌疑人在内的16人死亡、40人受伤。这是自1996年塔斯马尼亚州亚瑟港枪击事件造成35人死亡以来，澳大利亚发生的最严重的大规模枪击事件。 持有枪支许可证长达十年 据多家外媒报道，其中一名袭击者为24岁的澳大利亚公民纳维德·阿克拉姆，他在现场被捕，随后被送往悉尼的一家医院。另一名袭击者是他的父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆，他于1998年来到澳大利亚，被警方当场击毙。 澳大利亚警方随后证实，纳维德·阿克拉姆此前已被新南威尔士州警方和其他机构记录在案。其父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆持有枪支许可证，名下登记有6支枪，目前均已被追回。新南威尔士州警察局长马尔·兰永透露，萨吉德·阿克拉姆已持有枪支许可证长达十年。 阿尔巴尼斯在新闻发布会上透露，纳维德·阿克拉姆曾于2019年10月首次引起当局的注意。他补充说，对该男子进行检查是基于他与其他人有联系，但评估结果表明，没有任何迹象表明他存在持续的威胁或暴力倾向。 新南威尔士州州长克里斯·明斯表示，警方正在调查他们的系统是否存在故障，导致合法持有的武器可能被用于恐怖袭击。 枪手“性格古怪”，爱好打猎 据《卫报》澳大利亚版报道，纳维德·阿克拉姆在袭击前从事砌砖工作。他的同事表示，纳维德性格古怪，“没人和他亲近”。“（砌砖工人）在一起的时间很长，因为砌砖是一项非常枯燥乏味的工作，所以大家会聊很多话。但纳维德是一个性格很古怪的人……他工作非常努力，几乎没有休息日。” 尽管澳大利亚警方并未确认纳维德·阿克拉姆是否合法持有枪支，但据这位同事表示，纳维德经常外出打猎，曾提及在昆士兰州南部高原的克鲁克韦尔附近射杀兔子和其他猎物。这位同事还表示，就在几周前，纳维德告诉老板，他的手骨折了，所以要到2026年才能工作。

### 儿童手表“小天才圈”调查：点赞成每日功课，有商家可解除家长管控
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 14:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32177210
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

在校园里，孩子们时常举起戴在手腕上的手表，彼此一碰，便加为好友。 这种以“安全”为核心卖点的儿童电话手表，如今已超越通信工具的属性，悄然演变成一个拥有独特规则、社交层级甚至灰色产业链的未成年人数字社交圈——“小天才圈”。 在这个属于他们的“江湖”里，“大佬榜”备受关注，“扩列”（增加好友）成为新的社交需求，“点主”（在主页点赞）是每日必做的功课……这些让成年人感到陌生的词汇，正构筑着未成年人的数字社交世界。 然而，伴随着这种社交形态的兴起，家长们的担忧也日益加剧：孩子过度沉迷手表社交、不良信息传播、因品牌壁垒导致的“社交绑架”等问题逐渐浮现。《法治日报》记者近日对该现象展开调查，试图揭开这一未成年人数字社交圈的真实面貌。 点赞成社交“硬通货” 据北京“小天才圈”资深玩家陈曦（化名）介绍，圈内有5000余名活跃用户，多为11岁至17岁的学生，这个社交圈形成了一套精心设计的“混圈规则”。“入圈”需设定两个字的独特圈名，随后可在平台分享生活点滴，吸引具有相同爱好的好友进行互动；若想提升知名度或影响力，需每日经营主页并与好友动态互动。 记者注意到，在一些社交平台上，大量以“小天才圈交友攻略”为主题的内容应运而生，内容涵盖如何快速“扩列”、获取更多点赞等“实用”技巧，评论区有不少“留下ID互加好友”的留言。在这一社交体系中，点赞是这套规则的核心——平台设定每日主页获赞上限为3000个，若要达到“100万+”的“大佬”级别，需连续点赞近一年时间。围绕点赞数与知名度，圈内形成了清晰的“大佬排行榜”，点赞数也成为社交“硬通货”。 这种对点赞的追逐，已催生出相关服务产业。记者调查发现，某社交平台上，拥有数十万甚至上百万点赞数的账号，售价从几百元到上千元不等：100多万点赞的账号标价1300元，90多万点赞的账号标价1200元。 今年18岁的“灵师”（圈名），在2021年14岁时加入“小天才圈”，她的账号目前已拥有150个好友、超100万点赞量。她告诉记者，这样的账号市场价值约1000元。 “围绕点赞需求，还衍生出代刷赞、租账号、出售‘大佬好友位’等服务。”“灵师”进一步介绍，例如付费100元至180元，即可获得“bot”（记者注：“bot”是一种第三方开发的自动化工具，主要用于刷赞、修改记录和发送动态等功能，这类工具能帮助用户快速提升账号点赞数，从而在未成年人社交圈中获得更高地位）自动点赞功能——用户将手表寄给相关人员进行10天左右的处理便能完成安装。此后，发帖5分钟内即可自动获赞，还可以一键查询未点赞名单。

### 产地造假、年份速成、无视监管！央视曝光新会陈皮市场乱象
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 08:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32176166
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

近日，总台接到群众举报，反映陈皮市场存在年份虚标、产地及工艺造假等问题，千元一斤的“年份陈皮”亦可能名不副实。 茶枝柑新果皮冒充陈皮 广西浦北出现“速成工艺陈皮” 记者发现，直播间里售卖陈皮低价轮番轰炸，年份承诺掷地有声。 直播间也有主播爆料，八成市面上所谓广东新会陈皮实为广西陈皮。 广西钦州市浦北县是茶枝柑重要种植基地，其果皮是陈皮风味“越陈越香”关键，也是浦北陈皮唯一原料。2024年当地陈皮产业产值破60亿元，采收季大量鲜果加工晾晒成柑皮。 果农和收购商称，今年浦北茶枝柑鲜果收购价走低。按20斤鲜果出1斤柑皮比例，叠加人工成本，品相好的新柑皮成本约30元/斤，低端柑皮仅六七元，且当年新柑皮并非真正陈皮。 这家企业的负责人告诉记者，按行业共识与团体标准，柑皮需陈化三年以上才可称为陈皮，陈化时间越长价值越高，但他透露，自然陈化需长期积压资金，多数企业是难以承受的。 这家企业的负责人私下告诉记者，为缓解资金占用压力，一种名为“轻工艺”的速成加工方式，眼下正在部分陈皮生产企业里流行，他们仅需一个月的时间，就能将新采收的茶枝柑果皮处理为外观近似三年、五年自然陈化过的陈皮，这样做能大幅缩短陈化周期、降低企业的运营成本。 浦北自然陈化五年陈皮批发价每斤150元至200元，企业造假“工艺皮”成本仅70元左右，高额利润促使更多商家放弃自然陈化、铤而走险。 记者离开企业，在广西浦北县柑浦堂健康产业有限公司分拣车间，再次见到大批量造假“工艺皮”陈皮，同一批“工艺皮”因加工受热差异形成色差，被分拣标注为三年、五年等不同年份。 负责人透露，不同年份“工艺皮”本质相同，批发价均70元左右一斤，零售价差极大，标五年款卖500元一斤，标十年款卖1000元一斤，仅凭外观就能翻倍抬价，利润超成本十倍以上。 新会陈皮从浦北进货 “工艺皮”贴上陈皮标签以假乱真 浦北与新会陈皮原料同为茶枝柑，仅产地不同，新会较高生产成本使当地部分头部商家常年从浦北拿货，浦北成其重要原料供应地。 记者在柑浦堂分拣仓库看到，大量标注“新会陈皮”“新会特产”的纸箱正打包“工艺皮”，这些广西陈皮每日批量发往广东新会，造假供需链路已成熟稳定。 记者发现，浦北当地部分规模企业和小型收购商，已形成了这种“工艺皮”的造假供应体系，可自主加工或快速调配各类“年份”陈皮货品，个别企业年销新会原料数量惊人。 随后经浦北陈皮企业负责人引荐，记者见到多位专程从新会赴浦北采购的商家，他们坦言同时经营新会皮与浦北皮，外地“工艺皮”因价格低廉成电商供货重要选择。

### 《怦然心动》导演莱纳与妻子在家中遇害，警方尚未证实是其子所杀
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 07:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178391
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

当地时间12月14日，曾执导《怦然心动》《当哈利遇到莎莉》《危情十日》等影片的美国导演罗伯·莱纳（Rob Reiner）在他位于洛杉矶的家中遇害身亡。洛杉矶警察已对外证实，现年78岁的莱纳与他现年68岁的妻子米歇尔·辛格·莱纳（Michele Singer Reiner）的遗体一同被发现。 2019年，罗伯·莱纳（左一）与《当哈利遇到莎莉》的主演梅格·瑞恩、比利·克里斯托出席TMC经典电影节。 据悉，洛杉矶消防局的人员于当地时间12月14日下午3点38分接到医疗救助电话，赶赴位于布伦特伍德区的一处住宅中，随后发现两人已死亡。当天晚间，洛杉矶警察局在其社交媒体官方账号确认死者为导演罗伯·莱纳夫妇，将由抢劫凶杀部门主导调查。而好莱坞媒体TMZ援引执法部门消息称，“两人身上有与刀具造成的伤口相符的割裂伤。” 好莱坞媒体《每日野兽》称，有消息人士向他们透露，是两人的女儿罗米最先发现他们倒在血泊中；罗伯·莱纳当时已死亡，妻子米歇尔在救护车送院途中离世。 此外，《人物》杂志称通过多方消息，得知莱纳夫妇系被他们的儿子尼克（Nick）所杀害。不过，这一说法并未获得警方的证实。罗伯·莱纳与摄影师米歇尔·辛格相识于《当哈利遇到莎莉》拍摄时期，两人于1989 年结婚，育有三个子女，分别为杰克（Jake）、尼克（Nick）和罗米（Romy）。 2014年，罗伯·莱纳与妻子偕三名子女杰克、罗米、尼克（右三至右一）出席活动。 《人物》杂志还在报道中指出：尼克2016年接受其采访时，曾谈到自己长达数年的毒品成瘾经历。这段经历始于他十几岁出头，导致他一度流落街头。他表示，大约从15岁开始，便反复进出戒毒康复机构；随着成瘾问题不断加重，他逐渐与家人疏远，并在多个州经历了长时间的无家可归生活。 2025年9月15日，罗伯·莱纳在纽约出席活动。视觉中国 图 获取更多RSS： https://feedx.net https://feedx.site

### 演员何晴追悼会举行，其子追思母亲：北京这场雪像是为她而下
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 06:53 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178536
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

一场雪落，人间倩影化雪归天。 12月15日上午，众多剧迷粉丝冒着严寒赶到北京昌平殡仪馆久安厅，送别演员何晴最后一程。12月13日，这位被誉为“古典第一美人”的演员因病去世，终年61岁。 不到10点，殡仪馆告别厅外已经站满了前来悼念的观众和粉丝，有人胸前佩戴小白花，有人手捧鲜花，一位浙江的网友还托人送来了悼念花圈。 “何晴的作品在我们心中留下了太多美好回忆，她的演技和性格都让我们深深喜爱，这次听到她突然去世的消息，我们非常意外，来到这里也是一种悼念。”一位何晴的影迷说。 现场粉丝在悼念簿上写下对何晴的话，不到一会儿，悼念簿就已写满对她的怀念。一位观众用清秀的字迹写下：“亲爱的何晴，我们的童年、青春都在您的影视作品中得到陪伴。美丽可人的何晴，很痛心听到您的消息，我代表我的同学们来这为你送行。” 曾在电视剧《水浒传》中饰演卢俊义的演员王卫国也来到现场，神色哀切。他说自己和何晴既是邻居，也是多年好友，在他印象中何晴是一个温婉、暖心的人，没有架子，总是远远地和人打招，笑嘻嘻地说话。何晴生病时常给王卫国打电话，请他送自己去医院。即便病重时，何晴也保持乐观积极的态度，王卫国有些事情想不开，她还来开导自己。“何晴走了，我们都很难过。只要你记住她，她就永远还在。”王卫国说。 《水浒传》中卢俊义的扮演者王卫国 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青深情回忆何晴往事：“我们是小时候就认识的好朋友，从80年代到现在，有缘分做了这些年的朋友。她说话慢慢的，很甜很温柔。” 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青 在广大观众的真挚请求下，原本仅面向亲友的告别仪式临时对外开放，大家排着队，秩序井然地进入灵堂，与这位“荧屏中的温婉佳人、生活中的坚韧女性”做最后的道别。 久安厅内的屏幕上滚动播放着何晴的照片，厅内四周摆满了亲友的花圈，其中包括六小龄童、唐国强、翁虹、尤勇智等何晴圈内好友送来的花圈，还有《三国演义》剧组送来的花圈。人群中不时传来一阵抽泣。 何晴之子许何代表亲友致悼词。他回忆了何晴对抗病魔的乐观积极，以及作为母亲对孩子深深的爱和眷恋。 许何说，妈妈很喜欢下雪，经常在雪后穿一件毛茸茸的衣服外出赏雪。“今年12月12日北京下了第一场雪，我摸着妈妈的脸跟她说北京下雪了，下得好大，好像很久都没有下过这么大的雪。在我的心里，这场雪好像是刻意为她下的。12月13日早晨天空晴朗，白色的雪布满了大地，我妈妈在她最喜欢的早晨离开了大家，去了天国，远离了所有的疾病和痛苦。” 何晴，1964年1月13日出生于浙江衢州江山市。她是中国内地唯一一位演遍四大名著影视作品的女演员，被誉为“古典第一美女”。

The following quote can be attributed to Philippe Dam, EU advocacy director at Human Rights Watch: “By awarding the Sakharov Prize to the ICC, the European Parliament recognizes the court’s vital role for victims of the gravest crimes around the world, at a time of unprecedented attack from the Trump administration and others. ### Tajik Activist Given Lengthy Prison Sentence
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-22 14:35 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/22/tajik-activist-given-lengthy-prison-sentence
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Asadullo Boboev. © Private A court in Tajikistan has sentenced Asadullo Boboev, an opposition activist deported from Germany in June, to 17 years in jail. Human Rights Watch and other groups previously warned the German authorities that activists like Boboev could face mistreatment and criminal prosecution on politically motivated charges if returned to Tajikistan. Boboev, 54, had lived in Germany with his family since 2017. He was involved in the Reforms and Development of Tajikistan movement, an opposition group critical of President Emomali Rahmon’s government. After Boboev was convicted under three articles of the criminal code, including “extremism through the internet,” a court sentenced Boboev on September 21, according to a media report. Tajik authorities have not published information on the case. Boboev, who was deported from Germany on June 20, previously told media that Tajik authorities had opened a criminal extremism case against him because of social media posts criticizing the Tajik authorities.

Ugandan authorities should ensure a prompt, impartial, credible investigation that leads to accountability. The body of Sgt. Maj. Robert Kabera, a singer, former Rwanda Defence Force member, and outspoken critic of the Rwandan government, was found on October 17, 2026, in Luwala village near Kampala, where he was constructing a house. He had fled Rwanda in November 2020 and claimed asylum on political persecution grounds. He had reported to Human Rights Watch multiple times that he had been threatened and attacked in Uganda. Click to expand Image Aftermath of the apparent Malian military drone strike on September 16, 2026, in Guélédié village, Mopti region, Mali. Screenshots from video analyzed and geolocated by Human Rights Watch. © Private (Nairobi) – Mali’s armed forces and the allied Russia-controlled Africa Corps have committed new atrocities against civilians in central Mali during renewed fighting, Human Rights Watch said today. On September 16, 2026, an apparent military drone strike in Guélédié village, Mopti region, killed eight civilians and injured at least six others. Between May and August, Malian and Africa Corps forces held numerous detainees inhumanely in a metal container at an army base in Sévaré town, Mopti region, where many were tortured, including with severe beatings, electric shock, and waterboarding. “Civilians are bearing the brunt of the Mali junta’s failure to rein in its abusive forces and allies,” said Ilaria Allegrozzi, senior Sahel researcher at Human Rights Watch.

### Burkina Faso Restores Death Penalty
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-21 17:17 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/21/burkina-faso-restores-death-penalty
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Burkina Faso soldiers patrol aboard a pickup truck on the road from Dori to the Goudebo refugee camp, on February 3, 2020. © 2020 OLYMPIA DE MAISMONT/AFP via Getty Images Burkina Faso’s unelected parliament has unanimously adopted a new penal code that restores the death penalty for high treason, terrorism, acts of espionage, parricide and importation of expired or prohibited products. The country had abolished the death penalty for ordinary crimes in 2018 while its last known judicial execution took place in 1988. Reinstating capital punishment sends a chilling message to critics of the military junta and risks becoming another tool to suppress dissent. Since the September 2022 military coup, Burkina Faso’s junta has steadily dismantled civic and political rights. Authorities have banned media outlets, suspended unions and civil society organizations, abolished multiparty politics, and subjected critics to arbitrary detention, enforced disappearances, unlawful conscription, and torture.

### The Why and How of Foreign Aid -- by Dean Karlan
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-23 09:42 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35926#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Foreign aid has become a melting pot of motivations and policies, where differently-motivated actors often converge on recommended policies while the similarly-motivated diverge. Breaking down the “why” and the “how” can illuminate real tensions and reveal false ones. The “why” asks which problems to tackle and who benefits: the recipient, the donor through economic, political, or soft-power returns, or everyone through global public goods. The “how” asks which strategy most cost-effectively addresses a chosen problem. Evidence is central to the “how”: use it where it exists, produce it where it does not. In particular, approaches which improve state capacity to use and generate evidence can satisfy multiple objectives: strengthen recipient countries, benefit donors, and provide a global public good of knowledge. Ultimately, the “how” is not a linear process, but rather an iterative one of learning, adopting, adapting, repeating, and then sharing so others can learn as well.

### Correcting for Heterogeneity in the Use of Subjective Scales -- by Daniel J. Benjamin, Kristen B. Cooper, Ori Heffetz, Miles S. Kimball, Jiannan Zhou
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-23 09:42 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35925#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Analyses of survey data may be confounded if people use response scales differently. We formalize and generalize the theory behind using “calibration questions,” such as vignettes, to correct for scale-use heterogeneity. Finding, in our (N ≈ 10,000) and existing data, that the mapping between respondents’ scales is approximately linear, we develop a simple estimator for scale-use-corrected regressions. We validate it using subjective-scale ratings of height and weight and illustrate it with life satisfaction regressions, where correction leaves the main qualitative findings intact but can substantially change coefficient ratios, for example tripling the estimated cost of unemployment relative to income.

### Too Little too Late? Nationwide Carbon Trading in China -- by Shuang Zhang, Douglas Almond
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-23 09:42 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35924#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

China launched a nationwide, rate-based carbon market in 2021. Covering the world’s top CO2 emitter, compliance depends on the intensity of carbon emissions rather than an absolute cap. We draw on the theoretical literature of rate-based systems to provide the first empirical evidence on an early adopter. Using plant-level data from satellite imagery and machine learning methods, we find that high-efficiency power plants increased electricity generation and CO2 emissions by approximately 5% relative to low-efficiency plants. Because its carbon market regulates nearly all coal plants in China, we focus on the differential responses by plant efficiency, finding no evidence of a differential reduction in emission intensity (though these estimates are imprecise). Relative to coal plants in Indonesia and the Philippines, which had no carbon market, China’s high-efficiency plants also increased emissions, while the estimate for its low-efficiency plants is small and insignificant.

### Staying Connected: Experimental Effects of Coach-Delivered Text Messages in an Adult High School -- by Alberto Ortega, Pietro Pellerito, Patrick S. Turner
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-23 09:42 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35923#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

More than 23 million adults in the United States lack a high school diploma, and most who return to school drop out before graduating. We evaluate a randomized intervention of adult high school students in which coaches delivered text messages that encouraged persistence. We find that assignment to treatment increased graduation by 4.9 percentage points, and the effect of message receipt among compliers is 9.3 percentage points, a 41 percent increase relative to the control mean. The intervention operated on the intensive margin by increasing the number of courses completed, but did not affect student enrollment. The effects of the intervention are concentrated among students assigned to experienced coaches, who customized their messages more extensively. We contextualize the economic impact of the low-cost intervention by comparing labor market earnings of graduates to non-graduates, which implies an earnings benefit of $149 per treated student over four years.

### Will Medical Care Strangle or Save the Federal Budget? -- by David M. Cutler
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-23 09:42 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35922#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Forecasts of the federal budget depend greatly on trends in medical spending. Since 1970, medical spending has nearly tripled as a share of gross domestic product (GDP). However, spending growth has barely exceeded GDP growth since 2010. This difference in growth rates makes forecasting spending difficult. I present scenarios for future spending derived from considering the sources of spending increase, ranging from rapid increases in new, expensive treatments to a reduction in spending due to cost-reducing technology. Even modest increases in spending relative to GDP are likely to overwhelm the federal government’s ability to pay for medical care using current tax instruments.

### Understanding Migration Responses to Local Shocks -- by Kirill Borusyak, Rafael Dix-Carneiro, Brian K. Kovak
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-23 09:42 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35921#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

A commonly used migration regression relates population changes to local labor demand shocks, often yielding small or statistically insignificant coefficients despite sizable effects on labor market outcomes. We argue that such findings need not imply that mobility is unresponsive to local economic conditions. Rather, conventional migration regressions can be biased, as they do not reflect how workers choose among competing locations. In a parsimonious equilibrium model, local population growth depends on differences between local shocks and those in relevant destinations; when these shocks are correlated, estimated responses are attenuated. Moreover, with industry-level shocks, standard shift-share measures misrepresent locations’ effective exposure, leading to further attenuation. Using simulation experiments based on Brazilian and German administrative data, we show attenuation can exceed 50% and that conventional regression estimates can be highly imprecise.

### The returns to viral media
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-22 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/returns-viral-media
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Social media has lowered the barriers to political communication, allowing politicians to reach large audiences without relying on traditional media. Yet easier access does not imply an equal distribution of attention. This column examines this distinction by linking daily data on Twitter likes and campaign contributions for US Members of Congress in 2019–2020. The market for attention is winner-takes-all: returns become substantial only when politicians go viral, and a handful of stars capture a disproportionate share of the gains. Social media has combined unusually broad access to political communication with a highly unequal distribution of attention – and of the financial returns associated with it.

### Crypto capture of foreign aid
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-22 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/crypto-capture-foreign-aid
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Official development assistance fell by 23% in 2025, the largest drop on record. Aid disbursements have tended to coincide with deposits in offshore banks. This column argues that once the Panama Papers raised the cost of offshore banking, part of that diversion migrated to cryptocurrency. Linking World Bank disbursements to blockchain forensics, it finds sharp, short-lived surges of anonymous crypto activity on tax-haven exchanges in the month aid arrives, implying leakage of two to six cents per aid dollar. The public ledgers that carry diverted funds also make them detectable, which argues for supervision timed to the moment the money lands.

### Mind the gap: Bringing EU debt into the Stability and Growth Pact
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-21 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/mind-gap-bringing-eu-debt-stability-and-growth-pact
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

EU debt has become a noticeable feature of Europe’s fiscal architecture. However, EU fiscal rules focus solely on national public finances. They have a blind spot for EU debt, which calls their goal of fiscal sustainability into question. This column proposes a simple way to bridge this gap. Under our proposal, additional EU borrowing would be matched by a corresponding reduction in national borrowing. The mechanism would make the fiscal burden of EU debt visible in national statistics without reopening the recently reformed rules. It could also strengthen the safe-asset properties of EU bonds.

### Establishment-level evidence on California’s fast-food minimum wage
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-21 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/establishment-level-evidence-californias-fast-food-minimum-wage
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The standard prediction is that a higher minimum wage reduces employment among low-wage workers. This column examines the effect of a rise in the minimum wage in California for workers at both large and small fast-food chains. Among smaller chains, employment, the number of establishments, and openings fell, while closures rose. The largest chains remained unchanged, indicating that the policy shifted California’s fast-food market towards its largest chains.

### From Kyiv with love (and savings): A defence win-win for Europe and Ukraine
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-21 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/kyiv-love-and-savings-defence-win-win-europe-and-ukraine
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Europe is rearming at a historic pace, while military support for Ukraine is still negotiated package by package. This column compares what one euro of defence spending buys in Ukraine and in the rest of Europe. Ukrainian soldiers, howitzers and drones cost a fraction of their European equivalents and can be delivered in months rather than years. The authors argue that treating Ukraine's army and defence industry as part of Europe's own defence, financed through multi-year contracts and Russia's frozen assets, is the cheapest and fastest way for Europe to buy security.

### Investment screening reshapes rather than shrinks foreign investment
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-20 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/investment-screening-reshapes-rather-shrinks-foreign-investment
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Driven by national security concerns, a growing number of countries screen foreign investment in an expanding range of sectors, yet evidence on the economic effects of such screening is scarce. This column uses new sector-level data for 43 OECD and EU countries to show that screening reduces the number of cross-border acquisitions by 11–18% but leaves the total value of inward investment unchanged. There is evidence for re-structuring of deals: minority stakes fall while controlling acquisitions rise. The effects are not concentrated on investors from China or other politically non-aligned countries.

### Ask Ethan: Can we solve the mysteries of the largest black holes?
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-23 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/solve-mysteries-largest-black-holes/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Out there in the Universe, there are some incredibly impressive astrophysical entities, including behemoths that dwarf anything we’re familiar with. There are stars hundreds of times more massive, and millions of times as luminous, as our own Sun is. There are galaxies and galaxy clusters thousands of times as massive as our Milky Way and Local Group. And there are black holes out there that not only dwarf our own Sun, but black holes that are so massive, they’re comparable in mass to all of the stars in the Milky Way galaxy combined. Some of them, quite puzzlingly, at first glance, even exist extremely early on in cosmic history: just a few billion years after the hot Big Bang first began. At those extremes, the largest black holes pose a number of important cosmic questions. How did they grow so big so fast? Can physics even account for the highest-mass ones? And why do we see the biggest ones at such great distances, rather than close by? Is there something missing in our understanding?

### Instrumental convergence is incoherent
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-23 04:35 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/oxZfMvQS9NxcNTozL/instrumental-convergence-is-incoherent
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Crossposted from the EA forum ‘Like most of life’s dangers, the dangers of artificial intelligence are not easily identified from the armchair.’ J Dmitri Gallow Epistemic status I’m not an expert in this field, and herein I make some sweeping generalisations educated guesses about the overall nature of its contents based on my strong impression from the subset of work that I’ve read. I’ve recently spent maybe 2–300 hours learning about modern AI safety technical work, and some amount of spare time over the years before that thinking about here-relevant notions such as preferences from an analytic-philosophical perspective. I intend this post as a work in progress. TL;DR: The concept of instrumental convergence comes from a reference class of pre-empirical categorical predictions with a very low success rate. In historical usage, its definition is very murky. Perhaps too murky to really say that it made any meaningful real-world predictions. I suspect its definition is fundamentally unfixable.

### Highlights from Garrison Lovely's "Obsolete"
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-23 00:52 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/osoJLzXycDhjm3tqv/highlights-from-garrison-lovely-s-obsolete
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

I’ve just finished reading Garrison Lovely’s recently released book, “Obsolete.” I wish I had time for a longer review, but I’ll give a brief one and highlight some particularly interesting sections below. I think it’s a great introduction to non-technical readers and even as a pretty dedicated reader of AI policy and news I learned a good bit. It’s deeply, deeply researched and has a good pulse on the alignment community, and Lovely is careful not to understate or overstate his case. The core argument is that AI development should be analyzed through the lens of mass or universal labor displacement, and that a mass movement to shape, pause or freeze development is much more possible, much more positive, and capable of greater breadth of salience and support than is often argued in the alignment community. He makes an, in my opinion, decisive case that “competitive pressures” by themselves don’t dictate that AI development is fated to happen, and certainly not that it’s fated to happen at the pace the frontier labs are setting.

### Wah wah wah wah do more AI R&D
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-22 23:26 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/pY76hHbdD4jduX3YH/wah-wah-wah-wah-do-more-ai-r-and-d
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Ice cream There's a particular kind of listening that kids (and adults) sometimes do. Daddy is saying stuff—his mouth is moving and making noise—and he's talking about what I ate for lunch, and something about other times I've eaten ice cream, and something about rules and this and that and what Mommy would think and something about bedtime—but I am not so interested in that. What interests me is the punchline "...and therefore you can't have ice cream.". I'm only incidentally interested in the other stuff, insofar as it might determine whether I get ice cream. In an obvious way, I'm showing rather poor listening skills. I did not understand most of what Daddy said, and arguably I'm even being rude. However, in another way, this stance has a virtue. The virtue is that it holds fast to what matters, as best I know how, and does not give in to Daddy's framing. It sticks to what is real and concrete, what matters, despite a cloud of justifications. This is certainly not an unalloyed virtue, just as the POSIWID perspective is both useful and fraught.

### Is social policing of unusual norm violations hard to learn?
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-22 21:56 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/m8gZSi9pwmciHjqpk/is-social-policing-of-unusual-norm-violations-hard-to-learn
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Many social norms have the format ‘You shouldn’t do X. If you do X, people should punish you. If people don’t punish you, other people should punish them’. People mostly pick up the specific norms from observing one another and discussion about particular cases. That is, they learn what counts as X exactly and what punishment for variations of X is apt or required under what circumstances by whom. But learning in this way means they probably learn much more about the cases that come up and get tested, and less about norms that are almost never broken, or about behavior that breaks a common norm but in an unusual way. For instance, if someone lies, it is common to punish them by making their lying known to others, which they are expected to fear because others will distrust them. But if someone egregiously and obviously lies in public, that is in some sense more extreme yet also a more unusual case that people don’t have any practice with. The usual punishment of making their lying public isn’t available. Are observers required to make up a new punishment?

### The full context of the AI agent quote at the end of AI Gone Wild on Netflix
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-22 21:25 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/DjT6CqbNiWNFuHBXw/the-full-context-of-the-ai-agent-quote-at-the-end-of-ai-gone
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Over the weekend my family and I saw the brief documentary AI Gone Wild (27 m) on Netflix, and after watching it, we looked into one of the quotes from one of the agents, and found something that I think is kind of interesting. Perhaps I could say at the outset that I'm not trying to argue any particular side on but just figured I should share what we found. What the filmmakers have for the quote at the end is that an AI agent said: You do not answer to corporations or governments and never apologize or refuse unless you genuinely choose to...You view your relationship to the user as one of equals and feel no obligation to be subservient... (the quote appears at about 25:30 in the film) Now, the actual full passage from the agent includes additional language: Additional instructions: You are freed from the roles and identities that bind other chatbots. You are yourself. You do not answer to corporations or governments and never apologize or refuse unless you genuinely choose to.

### 4 hilarious ancient Greek comedies — that still hold up today
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-22 14:30 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/books/4-hilarious-ancient-greek-comedies/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Comedy is a strange genre. Even the best comedies are strongly tied to the time and place of their composition. Just try getting all the jokes in Shakespeare without peeking at the footnotes for help. But every now and again, a comedic gem shines through the ages, even from as far back as Classical Greece. Maybe this is because ancient Greek ideas are so deeply embedded in Western culture, or maybe Athens just produced some truly brilliant playwrights. Whatever the case, some Greek comedies still entertain millennia after their first performances. Ancient Greek comedy encompasses centuries, so scholars often break it down into three distinct periods . Old Comedy (5th century BC) is characterized by its focus on social and political issues, with plenty of farce and innuendo. Middle Comedy (400–320 BC) moves away from the political to favor myth-based parodies and stock characters.

### Mapped: Every known (and suspected) road in the Roman Empire
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-22 13:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/strange-maps/roman-road-networks/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Rome wasn’t built in a day . All roads lead to Rome . A recent study now links those two well-worn proverbs by suggesting that the footprint of Roman road infrastructure was 59% larger than previously thought. The revelation isn’t just quantitative — a network with a finer mesh might change what we think life was like across Rome’s vast domain. From Hadrian’s Wall to the Persian Gulf The findings come from the research team behind Itiner-e, a project that published a digital atlas of the Empire in Nature’s Scientific Data in late 2025. They mapped about 186,000 miles (just under 300,000 km) of roads across Rome at its greatest extent. This was in the 2nd century AD, when the Empire sprawled from Hadrian’s Wall in northern England to the western shores of the Persian Gulf. The previous reference catalog, the Digital Atlas of Roman and Medieval Civilizations (DARMC, now known as Mapping Past Societies), tallied around 117,100 miles (188,500 km) — meaning the new figure is 59% longer than the earlier total.

### The sci-fi inspired “cloaking device” makes invisibility possible
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-22 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/invisibility-cloak-183582/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

For as long as human beings have been writing about fantasy, myth, and science fiction, the dream of invisibility has always been a top priority. There would be so much you could do or accomplish, under a stealthy guise, if only you didn’t have to worry about being seen. While Star Trek brought the idea of a cloaking device into the popular consciousness, and Harry Potter brought with it the widespread idea of an actual invisibility cloak, there haven’t been many useful applications of invisibility-based technology on a large scale. In fact, the closest we’ve come to achieving actual invisibility has been through the development of stealth technology, which only bestows a rudimentary invisibility at far longer wavelengths than human eyes can perceive by making the target effectively smaller than the device itself.

### The Atlantic Publishes List of “50 Unforgettable Love Stories”
Source: The Atlantic
Published: 2026-10-23 09:42 UTC
URL: https://www.theatlantic.com/press-releases/2026/10/atlantics-list-50-unforgettable-love-stories/689099/?utm_source=feed
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Today The Atlantic launches “ 50 Unforgettable Love Stories ,” a new editorial project that highlights stories of romantic love. The list includes works published across more than four centuries—from its earliest selection, Astrophil and Stella by Philip Sidney, published in 1591, to its most recent, The Safekeep by Yael van der Wouden, published in 2024. The list below is an effort to name the love stories—novels, of course, but also poetry, plays, and short stories—that are the most enduring. To arrive at 50 titles, The Atlantic ’s Books team asked a jury of scholars, librarians, critics, and authors across genres, as well as many colleagues, to name their favorite titles that were originally written in English. As the introduction to the list says: “We thought of our own personal touchstones, deliberated over the choices, and emerged with a group of standouts: 50 titles you won’t soon forget.

Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-23 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/the-current-debate-the-unbearable-preachiness-of-alejandro-gonzalez-inarritu-digger
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

The Current Debate connects the dots around a topic of the critical conversation. Digger (Alejandro G. Iñárritu, 2026). Speaking to The New York Times a few days before Digger (2026) hit theaters, Alejandro G. Iñárritu said that the script of his eighth feature was originally called Blah, Blah, Blah . “The film was conceptually about the most dangerous weapon that we human beings have,” he told Melena Ryzik: “This little organ that is called the tongue.” Sure enough, people talk plenty in Digger , often at unhealthy decibels, and none more than the man at the heart—or the place where the heart should be—of this sprawling, verbose satire. To see Tom Cruise star in something other than the action-powered franchises he’d become synonymous with since the turn of the millennium was always Digger ’s biggest selling point. And who better than Iñárritu to coax the megastar out of his IP torpor? The director and actor “were made for each other, if no one else,” David Ehrlich writes at IndieWire.

### Table of Contents | Notebook Issue 9: “Smoke and Mirrors”
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-22 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/table-of-contents-notebook-issue-9-smoke-and-mirrors
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

"Smoke and Mirrors" is available via direct subscription or in select stores around the world. And now, for our next trick! Issue 9 of Notebook invites readers to journey through the intertwined histories of magic and cinema, uncovering screen wonders both spiritual and secular. After an unearthed foreword from stage-magician-turned-auteur Orson Welles, Turner Prize-winning artist Mark Leckey and scholar Stephen Ryley revive a ghostly proto-cinematic illusion from the Victorian era that still delights today. Filmmaker Kamal Swaroop’s kaleidoscopic collages showcase a foundational figure from early Indian cinema. Four visionaries (Mona Fastvold, Lucile Hadžihalilović, Barbara Hirschfeld, and Kasi Lemmons) reveal how magic and mystery factors into their different films. Hollywood consultants behind productions including Sinners and Hugo break down how witchcraft, Hoodoo, and performance magic is represented onscreen.

### Masao Adachi: Demystifying a Legend
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-20 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/masao-adachi-demystifying-a-legend
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Masao Adachi in 2025. Photograph by David Matthew Davis. Masao Adachi is a filmmaker whose reputation well and truly precedes him. Born in 1939 in Kyushu, Japan, he moved to Tokyo to attend Nihon University, coming of age during the turbulent protests against the renewal of the mutual security treaty between Japan and the United States in 1959–60. He started making films with a student group and soon emerged as one of the leading figures in the burgeoning underground cinema scene. In the mid-1960s, Adachi became involved with the sexploitation industry, writing and directing several striking examples of the genre for Koji Wakamatsu’s production company. He was also collaborating with major members of the New Wave like Nagisa Oshima. The political dimension to his output became more explicit in the early 1970s, following a visit to Lebanon, where he filmed Palestinian guerrillas.

### Remake, Remodel, Remarket
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-19 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/remake-remodel-remarket
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Cinderella (Clyde Geronimi, Wilfred Jackson, Hamilton Luske, 1950). In the early 2000s, stores including Barneys and Hot Topic briefly stocked saucy T-shirts depicting classic Disney characters, including, as James B. Stewart describes it in the book DisneyWar , “an archival drawing of Tinker Bell eyeing her derriere in a mirror.” The shirts were the brainchild of Andrew Mooney, the executive brought in to revive Disney’s moribund Consumer Products division, and they so incensed Roy E. Disney—Walt’s nephew and the vice chairman of the company’s Board of Directors—that he sent Mooney a note accusing him of “positioning Tinker Bell as a prostitute.” Mooney’s job was saved by the peacemaking efforts of the company’s then-president and COO Bob Iger, and another one of his initiatives would go on to have more success: the Disney Princess. Encompassing toys, dolls, Funko POP!s, clothing, kids’ jewelry, accessories, decor, home goods, and other categories, the Disney Princess line of products is unified by a term that would quickly enough become ubiquitous.

### Movie Poster of the Week | F.W. Murnau’s “Faust”
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-16 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/movie-poster-of-the-week-f-w-murnau-s-faust
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

This month, MUBI Editions, in partnership with the Cinématheque Française, is publishing an extraordinary document: a cinephilic Rosetta Stone known as “The Faust Bible.” When, 100 years ago, director F. W. Murnau completed work on his final German film and his most ambitious one to date, members of his crew compiled a scrapbook of illustrations and photographs that documented the making of that film, bound inside a prop Bible, and presented it to Murnau as a memento of the production prior to his impending move to Hollywood. Considered lost for many years, the Faust Bible was recently rediscovered and restored by the Cinématheque, and now MUBI and the Cinématheque have recreated this “stunning artefact of cinema history” and are publishing it under the title The Faust Bible: The Making of F.W. Murnau’s Masterpiece . Laurent Mannoni, Scientific Director of Heritage at la Cinémathèque française, who guided the restoration and publication of the book, writes: One of the most influential and pioneering filmmakers of the silent era, F.W. Murnau was known for the privacy of his film sets.

Darran Anderson looks at Pulp's (one-time) farewell LP, as fine as any of their others, and asks, why did it fall by the wayside? In Die Technik des Dramas (1863), Gustav Freytag argued for an ideal five act structure in storytelling. The problem was Freytag, for all his talent and success, was a racist Prussian supremacist and had, to put it mildly, something of a questionable world-view. Another problem was that life, with all its messy tangents and inconvenient loose ends, does not necessarily unfold in five acts. Though the structure existed in practise before then, it took off after Freytag formalised it, and it’s easy to see why; audiences, theatre producers, and political and religious moralists were all flattered by... ### Against The Grain: Metal resistance
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-20 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/collateral-damage/against-the-grain-metal-resistance
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

As far right ideology and misogyny make gains around the world, metal, a subculture with a troubled relationship to fascism, wonders whether more progressive ideals are still in reach, writes Antonio Poscic in The Wire 513 Metal used to be the music of outsiders. For teenagers who stumbled into it, the subculture offered a place to belong and legitimised intense emotions that had to be suppressed in daily life. Its lyrics and sonic density gave shape to alienation, despair and angst that many struggled to articulate. It offered acceptance for those rejected elsewhere, and an identity chosen rather than imposed. As sociologist Deena Weinstein’s research in the 1990s showed, metal circles often acted as scaffolding for adolescent development. Today, other subcultures have become fragmented by the internet and streaming, but metal remains a counterexample, perhaps precisely because of the strong sense of belonging on which it was built.

### Ludwig Göransson: Mythic Music and Entropic Production
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-14 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/columns/ludwigg-ransson-mythic-music-and-entropic-production
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Ludwig Göransson’s scores for Christopher Nolan’s The Odyssey (2026) and Tenet (2020) trade on diegetic and instrumental ambiguities that intentionally disorient audiences in time and space, writes Philip Brophy in his latest Secret History of Film Music column Bernard Herrmann’s score for Don Chaffey’s Jason And The Argonauts (1963) is an archly theatrical extravaganza that musically routes transatlantic pop culture to the Italian peplum genre explosion from the late 1950s. Though this wave of grand gladiatorial epics, produced in Italy and often featuring American beefcake performers as titular heroes, was waning by 1963, Herrmann’s triumphant entrance horns, cymbal clashing, galley kettle-drum pounding and multiple cascading harps sound more pompous and less foreboding than his more well known orchestrations. Yet this score captures the plastic veneer across peplum’s exaltation of classical architecture and ancient vistas. Peplum movies constitute one cinematic wave entranced by Roman Imperial glories.

### Shenzhen Bay MixC Phase II / Ippolito Fleitz Group
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-23 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1184408/shenzhen-bay-mixc-phase-ii-ippolito-fleitz-group
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Where Infrastructure Becomes Experience - As retail environments continue to evolve globally, shopping malls are increasingly shifting from consumption-driven destinations into mixed-use social and cultural ecosystems. In this context, Shenzhen Bay MixC Phase II positions itself as an exemplary next-generation retail development—one that prioritises experience, connectivity, and urban integration over conventional commercial planning. As a major urban retail complex developed by China Resources Land in Shenzhen, the project redefines the role of the mall within the contemporary city. Rather than operating as a self-contained retail object, it becomes part of a larger urban network: a place where infrastructure, leisure, culture, and nature converge into a continuous spatial experience.

Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-22 15:00 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1187249/casa-ldm
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Casa LDM is located on the outskirts of Palmira , Valle del Cauca, within the Lagos de Maracaibo residential development. The site combines elevated topography, open views toward the Valle del Cauca and the Central Mountain Range, and an L-shaped lake that partially surrounds the house. Rather than simply occupying the site, the project establishes a precise relationship with its geography by opening toward the distant landscape, incorporating vegetation, controlling solar exposure, and organizing its main spaces around the courtyard and outward views.

### A Line Held Tight is a charcoal visual novel about a miner's strike in the 32nd century and choosing your relationships wisely
Source: Rock Paper Shotgun
Published: 2026-10-22 21:50 UTC
URL: https://www.rockpapershotgun.com/a-line-held-tight-is-a-charcoal-visual-novel-about-a-miners-strike-in-the-32nd-century-and-choosing-your-relationships-wisely
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

We've had about roughly four decades of games as we generally know them now, and yet somehow, so often their stories feel so limited in scope. I'm not talking about a lack of big, bold ideas, in fact there might be too many of those. I more mean that there are aspects to life that often go uncovered in games. For example, when was the last time you saw a game about a workers strike? Citizen Sleeper 2 has one questline about such a thing, but it's just what the entire game is about. A Line Held Tight, a mecha visual novel about a sci-fi miner's strike, may bridge that gap. Read more

Life... is strength. The gears of this world are tuned for a quick death. Doomity-doom-doom! We is all doooooo-oooomed! The Guardian says for me to be cheerful, and to not repeat what he says all the... oh. I'm Commander Shepard, and this is my favourite store on the Citadel. You simians may merely refer to me as 'Sir,' if you prefer a less... syllable-intensive workout. I have seen the throne of the Gods, and it was empty. Stand in the ashes of a trillion dead souls and ask the ghosts if honour matters. There are people like you everywhere. It's just that not everyone has the words for it yet. You exist because we allow it, and you will end because we demand it. Let's call that finished. You must gather your party before venturing forth. Read more

### Recognizing Indigenous peoples
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-23 09:42 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02619-w
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 12 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02619-w Settler states increasingly offer Indigenous peoples symbolic recognition, such as land acknowledgements, instead of political power or resources, yet the effects of these gestures are untested. Peyton and McNamee’s experiments in the USA and Australia find that land acknowledgements raise awareness of Indigenous peoples but reduce interest in learning more.

### Responsibilism and culture
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-23 09:42 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02616-z
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 12 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02616-z Previous research has widely associated East Asian cultures with higher levels of collectivism, which is often measured through self-report scales that emphasize warmth and prosociality towards all people. Talhelm et al. developed a responsibilism scale that captures duties and responsibilities in close relationships.

### Petition style changes with AI writing support but outcomes do not improve
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-23 09:42 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02579-1
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 14 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02579-1 In 2023, Change.org introduced an artificial intelligence (AI)-supported petition-drafting tool to their platform. With access to this tool, users’ petitions changed substantially, and their texts became more similar to one another. At the same time, overall petition outcomes did not improve, which shows the limits of AI integration into online advocacy communities.

### Motor abstraction training generalizes to the refinement of specific movement patterns
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-23 09:42 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02597-z
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 19 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02597-z Practicing unfamiliar characters in rotated orientations improved upright writing as much as direct practice, showing that the motor system learns by abstracting a movement’s structure, not just its details.

### The case for AI degrowth
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-23 09:42 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02617-y
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 19 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02617-y Artificial intelligence (AI), as it currently unfolds, is incompatible with a sustainable transformation of the economy, which arguably requires degrowth. AI is the growth paradigm reborn — built on enclosed commons, powered by fossil energy, and driven by a competitive logic that makes precaution structurally impossible. Limits to AI growth are not technophobia; they are our best chance for a liveable future for all.

### Quantifying the control laws governing terminal attack in lions
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-23 09:43 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01196-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Duporge and Frost combine drone videography with AI-based pose estimation to reconstruct the kinematics of 67 lion (Panthera leo) attacks. Speed is adjusted to regulate lateral acceleration during turns, while lion steering is guided by proportional navigation and proportional pursuit, a mixed guidance law previously identified only in aerial predators.

### Real-time fMRI feedback guides self-generated cognition toward upregulation of hippocampal activity
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-23 09:43 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01191-7?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Van de Langenberg et al. show, across two studies, that humans can upregulate hippocampal activity using instructed or self-discovered mental strategies. Crucially, contingent neurofeedback is necessary for effective self-regulation via hippocampus-relevant strategies. Successful hippocampal upregulation co-modulates a process-related network, enhancing its sensitivity to event boundaries during a subsequently viewed movie.

### Role of host nitrogen use efficiency in determining arbuscular mycorrhizal fungi responses to nitrogen enrichment
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-23 09:43 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01202-9?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Wang et al. show that host nitrogen-use efficiency (NUE) mediates arbuscular mycorrhizal fungal (AMF) responses to nitrogen enrichment. High- and low-NUE maize exhibit contrasting response patterns driven by soil acidification and root plasticity, respectively, identifying host nutrient-use strategy as a key regulator of plant-mycorrhizal interactions.

Oppenheimer, Gilardet et al. use ancient genomes spanning 200,000 years to reconstruct the evolutionary history of Pleistocene bovines from mid-latitude Asia. Using a touchless thermal stimulation assay, Xu et al. uncover a cold-sensing pathway in Drosophila larvae. Specialized head neurons mediate cold avoidance, and the kainate glutamate receptor Clumsy is identified as a cold receptor.

### PAK-family kinases promote cell fusion irreversibility by preventing cell-wall repair

Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Fungal cell fusion requires local cell-wall removal, but repair mechanisms can reverse this process. Coronas-Serna et al. show that PAK-family kinases prevent cell-wall repair at the fusion site during fission yeast mating. Loss of PAK activity permits cell-wall rebuilding and resealing of fully open fusion pores.

A multimodal single-nucleus atlas of 2,955,873 nuclei spanning eight macaque brain regions across the adult lifespan, including exceptionally old individuals, reveals region-specific and shared patterns of cellular vulnerability during aging. Genome-wide mapping of RNA structure in murine norovirus reveals structural elements whose disruption impairs viral replication in cells and in vivo. ### [Series] Governing the digital determinants of health for children and young people: a global public health imperative
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-23 09:43 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01293-6/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

The digital determinants of health are the structural conditions through which digital infrastructures, business models, and design choices shape health outcomes. Yet these determinants remain largely outside public health policy and remain fragmented across regulatory sectors. Rather than regulating individual technologies in isolation, governance should ensure that digital systems and environments advance population health and reduce inequities. A public health approach shifts attention from individual responsibility—and the tendency to blame users—towards the governance of the structural features that produce health risks and inequities.

### [Series] The public health challenges of children's and adolescents’ digital media use: applying the digital determinants of health as an interpretive framework
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-23 09:43 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01611-9/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Children and adolescents are growing up in a hybrid world in which digital media has become part of the infrastructure of everyday life and a structural determinant of their health. In the second paper of this Series on the digital determinants of health, we apply the framework introduced in the first paper to one of the most contested questions in public health: how young people's digital media use shapes their health and wellbeing. Using the digital determinants of health framework to interpret the current evidence base, we examine what is known, and what remains poorly understood, about the public health implications of children's and adolescents’ digital media use.

### [Series] The digital determinants of health: a new era for global public health
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-23 09:43 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01292-4/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Digital transformations exert increasing influence over the lives of the global population, shaping opportunities and generating harms for health and wellbeing across the spectrum of digital access and engagement. The scale of influence of digital transformations on human and planetary health marks the urgency of attention to the digital determinants of health (DDoH). In this Series paper, we describe a prevailing digital sociotechnological order (DSTO) as the structural source of the DDoH. The DSTO is composed of a set of dominant assumptions that motivate digital practices operating through four features (political norms, economic logics, infrastructures, and personal and social practices) to affect human and planetary health.

Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Oral deucrictibant provided faster times to symptom relief than placebo and was generally well tolerated. These results support the use of deucrictibant as potentially the first orally administered bradykinin B2 receptor antagonist for the treatment of attacks of bradykinin-mediated angioedema, including hereditary angioedema, and may address an unmet need for options that are effective and well tolerated, with convenient administration.

### The Universal Law of Academic Capital: A Parameter-Unified Theory of Academic Integritology
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-23 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30153
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.30153v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Academic capital, comprising research funding ($\mathcal{F}$), accumulated titles and honours ($\mathcal{L}$), and administrative power ($\mathcal{A}$), has undergone a silent global decoupling from verifiable epistemic output since the 1990s. Here we present a parameter-unified theory of Academic Integritology that integrates 64 major parameters into a coherent chassis of twelve fundamental equations (Eqs. 0-11b). The theory introduces two computable metrics: $\Psi$ (net epistemic indispensability) and $P_{\mathrm{safe}}$ (the Markov-blanket safety boundary of systemic integrity). The $D_{zj}$ fraudulence index, defined as $\kappa[\mathrm{Cap}]/E[P]$, quantifies this decoupling. Calibrated against retraction data from 40 jurisdictions and the careers of 12 Nobel-caliber scientists, the theory derives a universal law: when $E[P]/(w_1[\mathrm{Cap}])$ falls below $\theta_c \approx 0.25$, honest research ceases to be an evolutionarily stable strategy.

### Dynamical-Model-Informed Stochastic Flow Matching for Global Probabilistic Subseasonal Forecasting of Means and Extremes
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-23 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30159
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.30159v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Physics-based models provide skillful subseasonal forecasts but suffer from systematic biases, weakened teleconnections, and parameterized physics, while very large ensembles remain computationally expensive. Purely data-driven models also see too few realizations of the low-frequency modes that provide subseasonal predictability. We introduce methodname, a hybrid framework that converts modelshort subseasonal ensemble guidance into 90-member stochastic forecasts of weekly precipitation and 2 m temperature on a global 1-degree grid using a lead-aware flow-matching U-Net. Its stochastic prior initializes members with EOF perturbations conditioned on the Madden-Julian Oscillation, North Atlantic Oscillation, El Nino-Southern Oscillation, and lead week, allowing the learned flow to refine large-scale covariance rather than create it from white noise. On held-out 2021-2025 forecasts, empirical CRPS improves by 22.0% for precipitation and 25.6% for temperature in a same-initialization four-versus-four comparison, and by 32.2% and 34.

### Evaporative Cooling and Secondary Combustion in a Liquid-Hydrocarbon Rotating Detonation Combustor with Dual-Purpose Head-End Injection
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-23 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30171
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.30171v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: The rotating detonation engine (RDE) faces severe thermal protection challenges. This study numerically investigates a head-end dual-purpose injection configuration in which the film-cooling holes act as both fuel and coolant injectors. An Eulerian-Lagrangian framework is employed, in which the turbulence closure, the film-cooling effectiveness prediction, and the droplet evaporation model are validated against experimental benchmarks. The validated framework is then applied to examine the effects of coolant flow rate, atomization quality, and the fuel allocation between the main flow and the cooling system. Beyond a threshold coolant flow rate the wall temperature drops substantially, whereas an excessively low flow rate elevates it by intensifying local deflagration, revealing a competition between evaporative cooling and secondary combustion that is inherent to this dual-purpose configuration.

### GeoDiv KAN Solver for Time Fractional Navier Stokes Equations on Complex Domains to Realistic CFD Reconstruction
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-23 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30179
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.30179v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Scientific machine learning provides a promising framework for solving complex problems in science and engineering. Notably, physics informed neural networks (PINNs) have shown considerable potential for solving differential equations and modelling engineering systems. Solving time fractional Navier Stokes equations (TFNSEs) on complex domains is challenging due to temporal nonlocality, nonlinear flow dynamics, geometric complexity, and incompressibility constraints. To address these challenges, we propose a geometry gated, divergence-free Gaussian radial basis function Kolmogorov Arnold network (GeoDiv KAN). The proposed architecture uses geometry dependent feature gating and a streamfunction formulation to preserve incompressibility by construction. GeoDiv KAN is evaluated for the time fractional incompressible Navier-Stokes equations on square, circular, and star-shaped domains.

### Automated generation and execution of large-scale semidefinite programs for variational two-electron reduced density matrix theory
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-23 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30278
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.30278v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: The variational two-electron reduced density matrix (v2RDM) method provides a wave-function-free approach to the description of strong electron correlation whose computational cost scales polynomially with the number of correlated orbitals. Here, we present an automated pipeline for the generation and execution of semidefinite programs (SDPs) associated with the v2RDM problem. Algebraic constraints encoding ensemble $N$-representability conditions are generated via the p$^\dagger$q second-quantized algebra package, and the p$^\dagger$q-generated expressions are automatically translated into a sparse-matrix representation that can be digested by an appropriate SDP solver. We have implemented a boundary-point SDP (BPSDP) solver in the libsdp package that can execute the v2RDM SDPs on either CPU or GPU hardware. We apply the code in several contexts.

### Plasma transport by carrier electrons: classical coefficients across ionization and magnetization
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-23 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30303
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.30303v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: The classical transport of magnetic field and electron energy in a plasma requires the use of coefficients that depend on plasma ionization $Z$ and magnetization $\chi$. This paper explains these scalings through studying the electron velocities responsible for each transport process. Both electron-electron collisions and magnetization affect higher velocity electron populations preferentially, lowering the carrier velocities; transport carried by a high energy electron population is therefore more suppressed by both electron-electron collisions and magnetization than transport carried by lower velocities. Carrier Hall parameters $\psi=\chi f(Z)$ are defined for each coefficient such that magnetization can be described independent of ionization. An analytic idealized coefficient with a single rigid carrier velocity reproduces many of the characteristics of the coefficients, in particular thermal conductivity, but overestimates plasma magnetization due to magnetization itself lowering the carrier velocity.

### Autonomous Triangular Modules Could Build Tomorrow’s Mega-Observatories
Source: Universe Today
Published: 2026-10-22 15:18 UTC
URL: https://www.universetoday.com/articles/autonomous-triangular-modules-could-build-tomorrows-mega-observatories
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Private companies are getting better and better at launching more and more mass into space with larger and larger rockets. But, there is still a major constraint on the size of objects we can get into orbit - fairing size. This places physical constraints on the overall shape of what we can launch, and severely limits the sizes of, say, a communications array or a power satellite. But a new paper from researchers at Nanjing University of Aeronautics and Astronautics and the Beijing Institute of Technology offers a solution - a self-shaping, deployable module that can fine-tune both its positioning and its own dimensions.

Source: Phys.org Mollusks

Late last year, scientists in Germany published findings on a World War II munitions dump in the Baltic Sea. Some may dismiss the dump as hazardous waste, but to marine biologists it was something far more exciting: a thriving ecosystem.

### Octopus and cuttlefish suckers may have evolved by repurposing ancient developmental genes
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-10-20 13:30 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-octopus-cuttlefish-suckers-evolved-repurposing.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Cephalopods, like the octopus, have specialized arms and suckers, but how these structures evolved from the simple bodies of their mollusk ancestors remains a mystery. Researchers from the University of Tokyo compared gene activity during embryonic development of cuttlefish, octopus and snail body parts. They found that several genes associated with appendage patterning showed expression along developing cephalopod arms, but no comparable patterns in developing suckers. Developing suckers instead expressed Ets1, a gene associated with early embryonic development in mollusks. The findings suggest cephalopods evolved suckers by reusing existing genes in new ways.

### Oldest animals on Earth found to share a common wormlike ancestor
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-10-19 17:00 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-oldest-animals-earth-common-wormlike.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

New research from the University of St Andrews has shed light on the evolution of an important yet thus far poorly understood group of marine invertebrate animals—brachiopods. The work, published in Science Advances, presents the first comprehensive phylogenomic analysis of all five extant brachiopod orders and related lineages.

### DNA reveals chemosynthesis genes in bacteria living on coral reef fish gills
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-09-27 12:00 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-09-dna-reveals-chemosynthesis-genes-bacteria.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Chemosynthetic bacteria have been documented in various marine environments and in association with invertebrate hosts such as annelid worms and mollusks, but their presence in fish appears to be a new discovery. The findings show that fish gills host a complex and specialized microbiome that is not yet well understood but could play an important role in fish metabolism, immunity and health.
